# B3: Design System and Themes in the Real UI: Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Put the approved design system into the real screen: six looks (three themes, three presets) computed by one look engine in the shell, a validator that keeps the three meaning colours safe, a theme-file format, three motion levels and three effect switches, the ambient effects of the new conversation, and the Appearance settings rebuilt on that model, all in one commit that passes every proof command.

**Architecture:** `src-tauri/src/themes.json` is the single source of tokens. `src-tauri/src/look.rs` is the only code that computes a colour: it applies a theme, the user's choices and the validator, checks 26 contrast pairs and returns the CSS variables, the motion level, the effect switches and any notice. The shell commands `state`, `preview_look`, `save_settings`, `save_theme` and `open_theme` expose it, and the engine now reports a failed settings write. The screen only writes a look onto `<html>` (`ui/appearance.ts`), draws the effects (`ui/Ambient.tsx`) and lists the options from the same JSON file; `ui/App.tsx` and `ui/Settings.tsx` move to that model in the same commit, and `ui/styles.css` is rewritten on tokens. B2's runner proves it: Rust tests in `look.rs`, UI checks in `tests/ui/appearance.check.ts`, and screen scenarios in `testkit/src/screen/app.rs`.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2024 (no new crate: `serde` and `serde_json` are already in the workspace), SolidJS and TypeScript 7 (native `tsc`), Vite (JSON import, CSS `@container`), Fontsource font packages (OFL-1.1, npm), B2's `cargo t` runner with its screen scenarios (Windows) and `ui` checks.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-faber-redesign-design.md`, section B3 (commit 4 of the commit order in B0), the memory table of B10 (M1 and M2), review finding 3 (the screens that call the old module move in the same commit), and the Round lines of the review log about B3. The approved design is `E:\Documentos\ProjetosIA\faber-notas\design-final\` (`index.html`, `tokens.json`, `notes.md` and the `telas-*` screenshots); this plan copies it and does not redesign it. Executors read the spec's B3 section first.

**This plan ends in exactly one commit** (Task 18). Tasks 1 to 17 leave the working tree uncommitted. Each task says which commands must pass when it ends; the full list passes only at Task 18, because the old screen and the new look engine cannot meet halfway: the screens that import the old `appearance.ts` (`App.tsx`, `Settings.tsx`) and the shell that serves them move together (Tasks 7 to 14), and the app must not be started between Tasks 7 and 14.

**The plan was written against a working copy.** Every line of code in it was compiled and run on Windows 11 in a scratch copy of the repository taken after B2: `cargo t --all` ran all three suites (`rust`, `ui`, `screen`; 189, 18 and 18 tests) with no failure, and a failure case of each new scenario was shown to fail. The Find texts of every edit were applied to the B2 files of that copy, one after the other, and the result is byte for byte the validated tree. The executor must not "improve" the code: if a block does not match, compare it with the file before changing either.

---

## Deviations from spec

The spec was checked against the code and the approved mock. Where it is wrong, silent or can be done better, this plan follows the code. Each line says what the plan does and why.

1. **`themes.json` is generated from `tokens.json` once, and the plan embeds the result.** The spec says "copied". The copy follows the spec: short hex written out as `#rrggbb`, the `brand-*` tokens deleted, the Portuguese names left to the locales, English keys, and a contrast table recomputed for the 26 pairs. It also differs in three ways. It adds what `look.rs` needs: `defaults` (each base's own text font, heading font and motion), `validator.ground-keys` and `validator.meaning-keys`. `user-controls` is flattened to English keys (`themes`, `presets`, `accent.swatches`, `grounds`, `text-fonts`, `heading-fonts`, `text-size`, `density`, `corners`, `motion`, `effects`), and `grounds` is a list of five ids, not a map, because the screen needs only the ids and their order. Its `first-run` list is not copied: the labels were Portuguese, it holds only ids that `user-controls` already lists, and the first-run flow is B7's.
2. **The UI imports the whole `themes.json`, not only `user-controls`.** Vite's JSON import can name a top-level key only when it is a valid identifier, and `user-controls` is not one. The cost is about 25 kB of JSON in the bundle; the screen reads only `user-controls` and never computes a colour.
3. **Fonts live in `ui/fonts.css`, not in `import` lines of `main.tsx`.** Fontsource's static families (IBM Plex Sans and Mono, Barlow Condensed, Atkinson Hyperlegible) are imported one subset file at a time, `latin` and `latin-ext` only. Its variable families (Archivo, Newsreader, Bodoni Moda, Geist Mono) ship every subset in one stylesheet, which would put Cyrillic, Greek and Vietnamese files in the app. `fonts.css` therefore holds the `@font-face` blocks of those four, written out once with only the two subsets and the family names the tokens use (`Archivo`, not `Archivo Variable`). A face still downloads only when a look draws with it.
4. **`Look` carries `appearance`.** The spec's `look()` returns the variables, the motion, the switches, the checks and the notices. The plan adds the appearance with every field filled in from the base (accent, fonts, size, density, corners, motion, effects), so the Settings controls can show which option is on without the screen working out defaults.
5. **`App` takes the first state as a property.** `main.tsx` already awaits `state()` to apply the look before the first frame, so it passes that state to `App` instead of `App` asking again.
6. **One alert for a failed save.** The spec says the first run and Settings each show the sentence. The plan puts one `role="alert"` toast in `App`, fed by the single `saveSettings` helper that Settings and Setup both reach through `onSave`. The language list and the hooks checkbox put their value back when the save was refused; the first run does not advance (`onFinish` waits for a successful save).
7. **Failures from the look engine are objects.** The shell returns `Failure { key, detail? }` for look errors so a sentence can name the field or the pairs (`{detail}`); engine errors stay plain keys. `ui/api.ts` `failureOf` accepts both, and `errorText` stays for the first-run guide.
8. **The effects keep out of the greeting.** The greeting of today's empty state is left-aligned in a column of at most 640 px (`.thread.welcome`). The loom is hidden when the stage is 920 px wide or less, and the flow line when it is 1140 px or less (the stage is the window less the 248 px sidebar). The spec's 920 px rule was not enough: the first design review of the screenshots found the flow line over the end of the Workbench heading in a window of 1280 px, the default size. The cost is that at 1280 px Workbench shows no flow line; from 1389 px it does. B5b, which gives the greeting its final layout, may move these limits.
9. **The sidebar's serif row style stays in Settings.** In the comp, only the Chat, Projects and Settings rows use the theme's navigation font (Workshop's serif); the new-chat button and the lists use the interface font. Today's sidebar has no such rows yet (the area switch is in the top bar), so `--nav-family` applies to the Settings navigation only, and the new-chat button takes the comp's card style.
10. **The Bancada flow line names vendors with the app's own words** (`vendorName`: Claude, ChatGPT), and only those that are connected, as the spec says. The spec's example text says "Codex"; the app calls that vendor ChatGPT everywhere else.
11. **More tests than the spec lists, each for a way the feature can bite.** (a) A screen scenario in which the write of `settings.json` fails (a folder where the temporary file goes): the look must not change, the alert must say why, and the same click must work once the disk does. (b) The new-conversation scenario also runs at 720x480 (the window's minimum) and 1280x800 (its default) and checks that no effect lies over the greeting. (c) A UI check for the hidden-window class and signal. (d) Direct Rust tests for the colour helpers and for `Appearance::parse`. The spec's own test list is all there.
12. **M1 starts the app with a connected vendor.** The spec says "no CLI running": `Ambient` mounts only on a new conversation with at least one connected vendor, so the scenario uses `claude_chat` (the fake CLI is connected but no CLI process runs). Its name, "with no CLI running, Faber stays within its memory budget", is unchanged so that the port test still finds it. M2 opens Settings once and applies the six looks in turn, because the gear toggles the screen. Measured: M1 about 108 MB (it was 89 MB before this commit, without the dust canvas and the web fonts), M2 about 137 MB, M3 129 MB; all under 250 MB.
13. **The port-completeness test checks containment.** B2's test compared the whole list of scenario names with the nine of the Node suite. This commit adds three scenarios, so the test now asserts that the nine old names are all still there, and a second test pins the six looks.
14. **The preview machinery is built and tested but not yet used by a screen.** `previewer` (every request carries a growing number; only the latest answer is applied) is the spec's data-flow item 4. Settings still applies a choice by saving it, as the spec says for this commit; B7 and B8 are the first users.
15. **`Engine::settings()` is new.** `create_window` reads the saved appearance to paint the window with the look's `frame` colour; the engine had no plain accessor for the settings.
16. **Words and documents.** `set.themeHint` has a new text (the old one described the Mono model); the heading-font control uses the short labels Narrow, Book, Poster and Same. `README.md` gets a bullet and a new idle figure (the old "89 MB" is no longer true on the new conversation), and `docs/design.md` gets a short subsection, "How a look reaches the screen". The roadmap's measurement of 2026-10-10 is a dated record and stays as it is. The spec's list of changed files names neither document.
17. **Two things of the comp's Phase 1 empty state are not copied.** The comp shows a "continue where you left off" list to the right of the reading column and, in Workshop, puts the loom in the corner below it. Phase 1 has no such list, so the loom of a 1280 px or wider window sits behind the right end of the opaque composer; it never passes behind text (the collision check of Task 15 measures the text of every block), and it is hidden when the stage is 920 px or less. The Workbench flow line is an animated pulse, so a picture may catch it anywhere along the line. The design review of Task 17 listed both; they are left as they are until the screen has the list, and the maintainer can reopen them.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include this section. Values are copied from the spec and from `AGENTS.md`.

- Write code, comments, docs and commit messages in English. Original code only; never copy from TL-ADE, TL-Control or TL-Orchestrator. The tokens come from the approved design (`faber-notas/design-final/tokens.json`).
- Do not use vendor names (Claude, Anthropic, Codex, OpenAI, ChatGPT, Gemini, Google) in product, feature or package names. Plain text such as "Claude" in a sentence or a label is fine.
- Never read, copy, store or forward CLI credentials or session tokens. Child processes never go through a shell. Nothing in this commit starts a process.
- Every text on screen lives in `ui/locales/`: English (`en.ts`) is the source and the other four (pt-BR, es, fr, de) are written by hand, never machine-translated. The engine and the shell return keys (`error.*`, `look.*`), not sentences. pt-BR keeps the names of the looks of R60: Sala escura, Oficina, Bancada, Manhã, Alto contraste, Cobalto. In English they are Dark Room, Workshop, Workbench, Morning, High contrast and Cobalt.
- The screen never computes a colour. Only `src-tauri/src/look.rs` does, from `src-tauri/src/themes.json`. Everything in the CSS that is a colour, a size, a radius, a duration or a shadow comes from a token.
- A refused or failing look, and a failed write of the settings, change nothing on the page and show a sentence. The page changes only after `save_settings` succeeded.
- Meaning colours are locked: no accent, ground or theme file may set or approach amber (hue 40), Claude coral (14) or Codex violet (245) within 24 degrees; the neutral exemption is a spread of 16 or less between the RGB channels.
- Memory: Faber with no CLI running stays at or under 250 MB in every scenario (the product limit is 500 MB). Telemetry stays off. `font-src 'self'` already allows the fonts.
- `unsafe_code = "forbid"` (workspace lint). Rust edition 2024; `rustfmt.toml` has `max_width = 140` and `use_small_heuristics = "Max"`; run `cargo fmt --all` after writing Rust. TypeScript is checked by TypeScript 7 (`tsc --noEmit`) and linted by `oxlint ui tests/ui`.
- Files use LF line endings, with three exceptions that keep the CRLF the repository stores: `AGENTS.md`, `docs/design.md` and `docs/roadmap.md`. This plan edits `docs/design.md` with a script that writes CRLF.
- No new Rust crate. The only new npm packages are the Fontsource font packages of Task 10, pinned to the exact version `5.3.0` like the ones they replace.
- Tests are the B2 runner's: Rust tests in the module (`cargo test`), UI checks in `tests/ui/*.check.ts` (`cargo t --suite ui`), screen scenarios in `testkit/src/screen/` (`cargo t --suite screen`). The deleted Node suite does not exist any more. Unit tests never spawn processes, touch real git or call a real CLI.
- On the maintainer's PC export these before any `cargo` or `npm run`:
  `export RUSTUP_HOME='E:\Apps\rust\rustup' CARGO_HOME='E:\Apps\rust\cargo' PATH="/e/Apps/rust/cargo/bin:$PATH"`

## Review Focus

Failure modes the spec implies but a straight build would not test. Each has a test in the task named at the end of the line.

1. **A save that is refused or fails.** A full or read-only disk, or a look the validator refuses, must leave the page, the controls and `settings.json` as they were, say why in the person's language, and work again once the cause is gone. Tests: Task 6 (the engine keeps the saved settings and restarts nothing), Task 11 (`saveAndApply` check: a rejected save leaves `:root` alone), Task 15 (the screen scenario with a blocked `settings.json.tmp`).
2. **A saved appearance from an older Faber, or edited by hand, never stops the app from starting.** The old Mono, Studio and Cockpit maps, a look that fails a pair and a missing value all give the default look (Dark Room), with the reason on stderr. Test: Task 4 (`a_legacy_appearance_falls_back_to_the_default_look`).
3. **A theme file from another person.** Over 20,000 bytes, not JSON, a wrong type, an unknown key, `var(...)` in a colour, meaning colours in `colors`, an accent that sits on a meaning colour: it is refused with the field named, or the meaning colours are dropped, and nothing is applied. Tests: Task 5 (the size limit, 23 refusals with their field, the dropped colours, the round trip).
4. **The smallest and the default window.** At 720x480 (the minimum the shell allows) and 1280x800 (the default), in each of the six looks, nothing sticks out sideways and no effect lies over the greeting. Test: Task 15 (`every look fits the new conversation`).
5. **The system asks for less motion, or the window is hidden.** The effective motion level can only go down, the dust loop stops, and the CSS animations of the effects pause with the window. Tests: Task 11 (`effectiveMotion`, `shouldRun`, the hidden-window check).

## File structure

```text
src-tauri/src/themes.json             NEW     the single source of tokens (generated once from tokens.json)
src-tauri/src/look.rs                 NEW     the look engine: validator, appearance, look, theme files
src-tauri/src/main.rs                 EDIT    mod look; FullState.look; SavedSettings; preview_look, save_theme, open_theme;
                                              save_settings validates; the window background is the look's frame
engine/src/store.rs                   EDIT    write() and save_settings() return an io::Result
engine/src/engine.rs                  EDIT    Core::save_settings returns a Result; Engine::settings()
engine/tests/engine.rs, account.rs    EDIT    callers; the failed-write test
ui/api.ts                             EDIT    Look, Appearance, Failure, SavedSettings; the new commands; failureOf
ui/i18n.ts                            EDIT    failureText; fromEngine takes variables
ui/locales/{en,pt-BR,es,fr,de}.ts     EDIT    the texts of the looks; the Mono texts go
ui/appearance.ts                      REWRITE applyLook, watchSystem, switchTheme, saveAndApply, previewer
ui/Ambient.tsx                        NEW     the effects of the new conversation
ui/fonts.css                          NEW     the font faces of the looks (latin and latin-ext)
ui/styles.css                         REWRITE the stylesheet on tokens
ui/Settings.tsx                       REWRITE the Appearance section on the new model
ui/App.tsx                            EDIT    start-up, saveSettings, the alert, Ambient, display heading
ui/main.tsx                           REWRITE awaits state(), applies the look, imports the fonts
tests/ui/appearance.check.ts          NEW     the UI checks of this piece
tests/ui/index.ts                     EDIT    imports the new checks
testkit/src/screen/app.rs             EDIT    the appearance scenario, the new scenarios, M1 and M2
package.json, package-lock.json       EDIT    the font packages (by hand and by npm)
README.md, docs/design.md             EDIT    a bullet, the idle figure, one subsection
```

## Before Task 1

Do these once. Nothing here is a task, and none of it is committed.

1. B2 is committed: `git log --oneline -3` shows its commit on top of `phase-1-base`, and `ls testkit tests/ui .cargo` shows the runner. If it is not, stop: this plan edits B2's files.
2. `git status --short` shows nothing of this piece. It may still show the maintainer's own pending edits (when the plan was written: `M .gitignore`, `?? .ignore`, and untracked plan files under `docs/superpowers/plans/`). Leave them alone; Task 18 stages explicit paths.
3. Export the Rust paths from Global Constraints, then run `npm ci` and `npm run build`. The Tauri crate embeds `dist/` at compile time, so every `cargo clippy --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` below fails with "frontendDist ... doesn't exist" until `dist/` exists.
4. Run the baseline once, so a later failure is known to be this plan's: `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && cargo test --workspace && cargo t --all`. Expected: every command exits 0 and `cargo t --all` ends with `N passed, 0 failed, 0 not run, 0 skipped`.

How the Rust tasks work, test first: the module's tests sit in the `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` block at the end of the file. Each step group adds the tests, runs them and sees them fail to compile because the code is missing, adds the code above the block, and runs them green. Do not change a test to make it pass. `cargo` warns about code that nothing calls until Task 7 wires `look.rs` into the shell; those warnings are expected and the warnings-as-errors run comes at Task 7. Run `cargo fmt --all` after writing Rust; it may reflow lines and that is fine.

How the edits in this plan work: a block headed **Edit** gives a Find text and a Replace text. Apply them in the order shown, with the edit tool, one at a time. The Find text occurs exactly once in the file when its turn comes; if it does not, the file is not the one the plan was written against, so stop and compare. A block headed **Create** or **Replace the whole content** is the whole file, verbatim.

### Task 1: `themes.json`, the single source of tokens

**Model: haiku**

**Files:**
- Create: `src-tauri/src/themes.json`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: a JSON file read by `look.rs` (Tasks 2 to 5, with `include_str!`) and imported by the screen (Tasks 11 and 13, `import themes from '../src-tauri/src/themes.json'`). Top-level keys: `about`, `default` (`"sala"`), `css` (`base`: 98 tokens named without the leading `--`; `themes`: `sala`, `oficina`, `bancada`; `presets`: `manha`, `alto`, `cobalto`; each theme or preset lists only the tokens it overrides), `defaults` (per look: `textFont`, `headingFont`, `motion`), `locked` (the three meaning colours and their hues), `user-controls`, `validator`, `theme-file` (`extension` `.faber-theme.json`, `max-bytes` 20000, a documented `shape`) and `contrast` (per look, the 26 pairs and their ratios).

**Proof commands that must pass at the end:** the check of Step 2. Nothing else is affected yet.

The file was generated once from the approved `faber-notas/design-final/tokens.json` (Deviation 1). Do not regenerate it: copy it as it is.

- [ ] **Step 1: Create the file**

      "meaning": "Codex is working on it",
      "hue": 245,
      "shape": "diamond"

    "rule": "A ground may shift their tone (darker on light grounds). Their hue family never changes, they are never reused for ordinary roles, and a theme file can never set them."
  },

      "archivo": "\"Archivo\",system-ui,sans-serif",

      "atkinson": "\"Atkinson Hyperlegible\",system-ui,sans-serif"
    },
    "heading-fonts": {
      "narrow": {
        "font-display": "\"Archivo\",system-ui,sans-serif",

        "em-style": "normal",

      },
      "text": {
        "font-display": "var(--font-ui)",

        "display-tracking": "-.02em",
        "em-style": "normal",
        "em-color": "var(--accent-text)"
      }
    },
    "text-size": {
      "min": 13,
      "max": 18,
      "step": 0.5,
      "unit": "px",
      "drives": "--fs-ui; every interface size is calc(var(--fs-ui) +/- n px), reading text is --fs-ui + 1.5px"
    },
    "density": [
      1,
      0.8
    ],
    "corners": [
      0,
      1,
      1.6
    ],
    "motion": {
      "full": "slides and glows, ambient effect on the new conversation",
      "reduced": "short fades only (120ms), nothing loops or travels; forced when the system asks for less motion",
      "none": "no transitions, no animation"
    },
    "effects": {
      "ambient": "ambient effect on the new conversation",
      "texture": "grain and bench grid",
      "glow": "focus and amber light"
    }
  },
  "validator": {
    "used-by": [
      "accent swatches",
      "custom colour picker",
      "theme file import"
    ],
    "accent-hue-block": {
      "near": {
        "amber": 40,
        "claude": 14,
        "codex": 245
      },
      "within-degrees": 24,
      "neutral-spread": 16,
      "neutral-exemption": "RGB channel spread under 16 (of 255) has no hue, so warm white #f3f0e8 passes"
    },
    "contrast-fix": {
      "accent-min": 3,
      "accent-text-min": 4.5,
      "method": "mix toward white on dark grounds, black on light grounds, 5% steps",
      "re-check": "the corrected fill and text are checked against the hue block again; a new collision is rejected"
    },
    "accent-ink": "#000000 or #ffffff, whichever reads better on the accent",
    "required-pairs": {
      "plain-inks": [
        "ink",
        "ink-2",
        "ink-3"
      ],
      "meaning-inks": [
        "amber-ink",
        "claude-ink",
        "codex-ink"
      ],
      "surfaces": [
        "bg",
        "s1",
        "s2",
        "s3"
      ],
      "ink-min": 4.5,
      "accent": [
        "accent",
        "bg",
        3
      ],
      "accent-ink": [
        "accent-ink",
        "accent",
        4.5
      ]
    },
    "apply": "blocked while any required pair fails; the preview names the failing pair",
    "ground-keys": [
      "frame",
      "bg",
      "s1",
      "s2",
      "s3",
      "line",
      "line-2",
      "line-3",
      "ink",
      "ink-2",
      "ink-3",
      "amber",
      "amber-ink",
      "amber-on",
      "claude",
      "claude-ink",
      "codex",
      "codex-ink",
      "danger",
      "chart-rest",
      "stage-shadow",
      "card-shadow",
      "lift-shadow"
    ],
    "meaning-keys": [
      "amber",
      "amber-ink",
      "amber-on",
      "claude",
      "claude-ink",
      "codex",
      "codex-ink",
      "danger"
    ],
    "custom-ground": "when colors change bg, the meaning colours come from Sala (white text reads better) or Manhã (black text reads better), and the three plain inks are pushed toward white or black in 5% steps until they pass on bg"
  },
  "theme-file": {
    "extension": ".faber-theme.json",

      "format": "faber-theme",

    }

  console.error('FAIL', message);

};

}

if (JSON.stringify(Object.keys(t.defaults)) !== JSON.stringify(looks)) fail('defaults keys');
if (JSON.stringify(Object.keys(t.contrast).slice(1)) !== JSON.stringify(looks)) fail('contrast keys');

if (JSON.stringify([...uc.themes, ...uc.presets]) !== JSON.stringify(looks)) fail('user-controls themes and presets');
if (!Array.isArray(uc.grounds) || uc.grounds.length !== 5) fail('user-controls.grounds must be a list of 5');
if (t['theme-file']['max-bytes'] !== 20000) fail('theme-file max-bytes');

//! The look engine: it turns the user's appearance choices into CSS variables. Every colour rule lives here, so the
//! screen never computes a colour and `cargo test` covers the rules with no window. The tokens come from
//! `themes.json` (docs/design.md, "Looks").

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn rgb(h: &str) -> Rgb {
        parse_hex(h).expect("a test colour")
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_hue_block_passes_its_boundaries_and_refuses_just_inside_the_bands() {
        for h in [64.0, 221.0, 269.0, 350.0, 349.0, 100.0] {
            assert!(hue_block(h).is_none(), "{h} should pass");
        }
        for h in [63.9, 221.1, 268.9, 350.1, 355.0, 5.0, 0.0] {
            assert!(hue_block(h).is_some(), "{h} should be refused");
        }
        // The bands of Claude and amber join: 39 is 25 from Claude and 1 from amber.
        assert_eq!(hue_block(39.0).unwrap().key, "error.look.nearAmber");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_hue_inside_two_bands_names_the_nearest_meaning_colour() {
        assert_eq!(hue_block(20.0).unwrap().key, "error.look.nearClaude");
        assert_eq!(hue_block(30.0).unwrap().key, "error.look.nearAmber");
        assert_eq!(hue_block(27.0).unwrap().key, "error.look.nearAmber", "a tie goes to amber");
        assert_eq!(hue_block(240.0).unwrap().key, "error.look.nearCodex");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_hue_block_on_real_colours() {
        let key = |c: &str| near_meaning(rgb(c)).map(|f| f.key);
        assert_eq!(key("#f2b13c").as_deref(), Some("error.look.nearAmber"));
        assert_eq!(key("#e0673a").as_deref(), Some("error.look.nearClaude"));
        assert_eq!(key("#8f87ea").as_deref(), Some("error.look.nearCodex"));
        assert!(key("#5a4a2a").is_some(), "a dark olive brown is amber's hue");
        for pass in ["#5fb36a", "#4f9dde", "#f3f0e8"] {
            assert_eq!(key(pass), None, "{pass}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_hex_colour_is_six_digits_after_a_hash_and_the_wcag_ratio_and_the_fix_behave() {
        assert_eq!(parse_hex("#a1B2c3"), Some([0xa1, 0xb2, 0xc3]));
        for bad in ["#abc", "a1b2c3", "#12345g", "#1234567", ""] {
            assert_eq!(parse_hex(bad), None, "{bad:?}");
        }
        assert_eq!(hex([1, 2, 255]), "#0102ff");
        assert!((ratio(WHITE, BLACK) - 21.0).abs() < 1e-9);
        assert_eq!(ratio(rgb("#555555"), rgb("#555555")), 1.0);
        // The fix moves in 5% steps toward the target, leaves a colour that already passes alone, and stops at the target.
        let grey = rgb("#555555");
        let fixed = fix(grey, &[BLACK], 4.5, WHITE);
        assert!(ratio(fixed, BLACK) >= 4.5 && fixed != grey);
        assert_eq!(fix(WHITE, &[BLACK], 4.5, WHITE), WHITE);
        assert_eq!(fix(grey, &[BLACK], 100.0, WHITE), WHITE);
    }
}

```rust
//! screen never computes a colour and `cargo test` covers the rules with no window. The tokens come from
//! `themes.json` (docs/design.md, "Looks").

use serde::Serialize;
use serde_json::{Map, Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::sync::OnceLock;

type Obj = Map<String, Value>;
type Vars = BTreeMap<String, String>;
type Rgb = [u8; 3];

const WHITE: Rgb = [255, 255, 255];
const BLACK: Rgb = [0, 0, 0];
/// What the accent fill and its text version must reach on `bg`.
const ACCENT_MIN: f64 = 3.0;
const ACCENT_TEXT_MIN: f64 = 4.5;
/// Colours the theme file may set. The meaning colours (amber, Claude, Codex) are never among them.
const SOLID_COLORS: [&str; 8] = ["frame", "bg", "s1", "s2", "s3", "ink", "ink-2", "ink-3"];
const LINE_COLORS: [&str; 3] = ["line", "line-2", "line-3"];
/// The meaning colours in the order that settles a tie, with the error each one gives.
const MEANINGS: [(&str, &str); 3] =
    [("amber", "error.look.nearAmber"), ("claude", "error.look.nearClaude"), ("codex", "error.look.nearCodex")];

/// An error the screen can show: a translation key and, sometimes, the field or the pairs it is about.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Failure {
    pub key: String,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub detail: Option<String>,
}

impl Failure {
    fn key(key: &str) -> Self {
        Failure { key: key.into(), detail: None }
    }

    fn with(key: &str, detail: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Failure { key: key.into(), detail: Some(detail.into()) }
    }

    fn invalid(field: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Failure::with("error.look.fileInvalid", field)
    }
}

/// Engine errors are plain keys.
impl From<String> for Failure {
    fn from(key: String) -> Self {
        Failure { key, detail: None }
    }
}

// ---- the tokens ----

fn token(pointer: &str) -> &'static Value {
    static TOKENS: OnceLock<Value> = OnceLock::new();
    TOKENS
        .get_or_init(|| serde_json::from_str(include_str!("themes.json")).expect("themes.json is valid JSON"))
        .pointer(pointer)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("themes.json has no {pointer}"))
}

fn strings(v: &Value) -> Vars {
    v.as_object().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(|(k, v)| Some((k.clone(), v.as_str()?.to_string()))).collect()
}

fn list(pointer: &str) -> Vec<&'static str> {
    token(pointer).as_array().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(Value::as_str).collect()
}

fn number(v: &Value) -> f64 {
    v.as_f64().expect("themes.json number")
}

/// Every token of a theme or preset: the Sala base with the overrides of `id` on top. None for an unknown id.
fn css_of(id: &str) -> Option<Vars> {
    let over = token("/css/themes").get(id).or_else(|| token("/css/presets").get(id))?;
    let mut vars = strings(token("/css/base"));
    vars.extend(strings(over));
    Some(vars)
}

fn default_base() -> &'static str {
    token("/default").as_str().expect("themes.json default")
}

/// Ids of the themes and presets, in the order the screen lists them.
fn bases() -> Vec<&'static str> {
    ["/user-controls/themes", "/user-controls/presets"].iter().flat_map(|p| list(p)).collect()
}

/// The keys of an object in `themes.json`: the allowed values of a choice.
fn choices(pointer: &str) -> Vec<&'static str> {
    token(pointer).as_object().into_iter().flatten().map(|(k, _)| k.as_str()).collect()
}

pub fn theme_extension() -> &'static str {
    token("/theme-file/extension").as_str().expect("themes.json extension")
}

pub fn max_theme_bytes() -> u64 {
    token("/theme-file/max-bytes").as_u64().expect("themes.json max-bytes")
}

// ---- colours ----

fn parse_hex(s: &str) -> Option<Rgb> {
    let digits = s.strip_prefix('#').filter(|d| d.len() == 6 && d.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_hexdigit()))?;
    let channel = |i: usize| u8::from_str_radix(&digits[i..i + 2], 16).ok();
    Some([channel(0)?, channel(2)?, channel(4)?])
}

fn hex(c: Rgb) -> String {
    format!("#{:02x}{:02x}{:02x}", c[0], c[1], c[2])
}

fn rgb_of(vars: &Vars, key: &str) -> Rgb {
    vars.get(key).and_then(|v| parse_hex(v)).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{key} is not #rrggbb"))
}

fn luminance(c: Rgb) -> f64 {
    let [r, g, b] = c.map(|v| {
        let v = f64::from(v) / 255.0;
        if v <= 0.03928 { v / 12.92 } else { ((v + 0.055) / 1.055).powf(2.4) }
    });
    0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * b
}

/// The WCAG contrast ratio.
fn ratio(a: Rgb, b: Rgb) -> f64 {
    let (x, y) = (luminance(a), luminance(b));
    (x.max(y) + 0.05) / (x.min(y) + 0.05)
}

fn mix(a: Rgb, b: Rgb, t: f64) -> Rgb {
    let channel = |i: usize| (f64::from(a[i]) + (f64::from(b[i]) - f64::from(a[i])) * t).round().clamp(0.0, 255.0) as u8;
    [channel(0), channel(1), channel(2)]
}

/// Moves `c` toward `to` in 5% steps until it reaches `min` on every surface in `on`, stopping at `to`.
fn fix(c: Rgb, on: &[Rgb], min: f64, to: Rgb) -> Rgb {
    (0..=20).map(|step| mix(c, to, f64::from(step) / 20.0)).find(|x| on.iter().all(|surface| ratio(*x, *surface) >= min)).unwrap_or(to)
}

/// Pure white or pure black, whichever reads better on `bg`.
fn white_reads_better(bg: Rgb) -> bool {
    ratio(bg, WHITE) >= ratio(bg, BLACK)
}

/// The HSL hue in degrees. Only for colours that have one (see `near_meaning`).
fn hue(c: Rgb) -> f64 {
    let [r, g, b] = c.map(|v| f64::from(v) / 255.0);
    let (max, min) = (r.max(g).max(b), r.min(g).min(b));
    let d = max - min;
    let h = if max == r {
        ((g - b) / d) % 6.0
    } else if max == g {
        (b - r) / d + 2.0
    } else {
        (r - g) / d + 4.0
    };
    (h * 60.0 + 360.0) % 360.0
}

/// The hue block, as a pure function of the hue: the error for the nearest meaning colour, if the hue is less than
/// the allowed distance from any of them, measured the short way round the circle.
fn hue_block(h: f64) -> Option<Failure> {
    let block = token("/validator/accent-hue-block");
    let within = number(&block["within-degrees"]);
    let mut nearest: Option<(f64, &str)> = None;
    for (name, key) in MEANINGS {
        let apart = (h - number(&block["near"][name])).abs();
        let d = apart.min(360.0 - apart);
        if d < within && nearest.is_none_or(|(best, _)| d < best) {
            nearest = Some((d, key));
        }
    }
    nearest.map(|(_, key)| Failure::key(key))
}

/// A colour whose channels are close together has no hue to confuse with a meaning colour.
fn near_meaning(c: Rgb) -> Option<Failure> {
    let spread = c.iter().max().copied().unwrap_or(0) - c.iter().min().copied().unwrap_or(0);
    if f64::from(spread) < number(token("/validator/accent-hue-block/neutral-spread")) {
        return None;
    }
    hue_block(hue(c))
}

struct Accent {
    fill: Rgb,
    text: Rgb,
    ink: Rgb,
}

/// The validator for an accent: swatches, the colour picker and theme files all come through here.
fn accent_for(candidate: Rgb, bg: Rgb) -> Result<Accent, Failure> {
    if let Some(f) = near_meaning(candidate) {
        return Err(f);
    }
    let to = if white_reads_better(bg) { WHITE } else { BLACK };
    let fill = fix(candidate, &[bg], ACCENT_MIN, to);
    let text = fix(fill, &[bg], ACCENT_TEXT_MIN, to);
    // Fixing the contrast can move the hue by a rounding step, so the result is checked once more.
    if let Some(f) = near_meaning(fill).or_else(|| near_meaning(text)) {
        return Err(f);
    }
    let ink = if ratio(fill, BLACK) >= ratio(fill, WHITE) { BLACK } else { WHITE };
    Ok(Accent { fill, text, ink })
}

#[cfg(test)]
```

}

// ---- the appearance ----

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Effects {
    pub ambient: bool,
    pub texture: bool,
    pub glow: bool,
}

const ALL_EFFECTS: Effects = Effects { ambient: true, texture: true, glow: true };

/// The choices behind a look. The saved appearance and the theme file share this shape; only `base` is required.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct Appearance {
    pub base: String,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub ground: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub accent: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub text_font: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub heading_font: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub text_size: Option<f64>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub density: Option<f64>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub corners: Option<f64>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub motion: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub effects: Option<Effects>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "BTreeMap::is_empty")]
    pub colors: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

impl Appearance {
    /// A theme or preset exactly as designed.
    pub fn of(base: &str) -> Self {
        Appearance {
            base: base.into(),
            ground: None,
            accent: None,
            text_font: None,
            heading_font: None,
            text_size: None,
            density: None,
            corners: None,
            motion: None,
            effects: None,
            colors: BTreeMap::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Reads the saved shape, or with `header` the theme file's three extra fields as well. Every problem names the
    /// field. The meaning colours in `colors` are dropped, not refused.
    pub fn parse(map: &Obj, header: bool) -> Result<Self, Failure> {
        const FIELDS: [&str; 11] =
            ["base", "ground", "accent", "textFont", "headingFont", "textSize", "density", "corners", "motion", "effects", "colors"];
        if let Some(unknown) =
            map.keys().find(|k| !FIELDS.contains(&k.as_str()) && !(header && ["format", "version", "name"].contains(&k.as_str())))
        {
            return Err(Failure::invalid(unknown));
        }
        let text = |field: &str, allowed: &[&str]| -> Result<Option<String>, Failure> {
            match map.get(field) {
                None => Ok(None),
                Some(Value::String(s)) if allowed.contains(&s.as_str()) => Ok(Some(s.clone())),
                Some(_) => Err(Failure::invalid(field)),
            }
        };
        let numeric = |field: &str, ok: &dyn Fn(f64) -> bool| -> Result<Option<f64>, Failure> {
            match map.get(field) {
                None => Ok(None),
                Some(v) => v.as_f64().filter(|n| ok(*n)).map(Some).ok_or_else(|| Failure::invalid(field)),
            }
        };
        let one_of = |options: Vec<f64>| move |n: f64| options.iter().any(|o| (o - n).abs() < 1e-9);
        let size = token("/user-controls/text-size");
        let (min, max, step) = (number(&size["min"]), number(&size["max"]), number(&size["step"]));
        let on_step = move |n: f64| (min..=max).contains(&n) && ((n - min) / step - ((n - min) / step).round()).abs() < 1e-9;

        let base = text("base", &bases())?.ok_or_else(|| Failure::invalid("base"))?;
        let accent = match map.get("accent") {
            None => None,
            Some(Value::String(s)) if parse_hex(s).is_some() => Some(s.to_ascii_lowercase()),
            Some(_) => return Err(Failure::invalid("accent")),
        };
        let numbers = |pointer: &str| token(pointer).as_array().into_iter().flatten().map(number).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        Ok(Appearance {
            base,
            ground: text("ground", &list("/user-controls/grounds"))?,
            accent,
            text_font: text("textFont", &choices("/user-controls/text-fonts"))?,
            heading_font: text("headingFont", &choices("/user-controls/heading-fonts"))?,
            text_size: numeric("textSize", &on_step)?,
            density: numeric("density", &one_of(numbers("/user-controls/density")))?,
            corners: numeric("corners", &one_of(numbers("/user-controls/corners")))?,
            motion: text("motion", &choices("/user-controls/motion"))?,
            effects: parse_effects(map.get("effects"))?,
            colors: parse_colors(map.get("colors"))?,
        })
    }

    pub fn to_map(&self) -> Obj {
        match serde_json::to_value(self) {
            Ok(Value::Object(map)) => map,
            _ => Obj::new(),
        }
    }
}

fn parse_effects(value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<Option<Effects>, Failure> {
    let Some(value) = value else { return Ok(None) };
    let Value::Object(map) = value else { return Err(Failure::invalid("effects")) };
    if let Some(unknown) = map.keys().find(|k| !["ambient", "texture", "glow"].contains(&k.as_str())) {
        return Err(Failure::invalid(format!("effects.{unknown}")));
    }
    let flag = |name: &str| match map.get(name) {
        None => Ok(true),
        Some(Value::Bool(b)) => Ok(*b),
        Some(_) => Err(Failure::invalid(format!("effects.{name}"))),
    };
    Ok(Some(Effects { ambient: flag("ambient")?, texture: flag("texture")?, glow: flag("glow")? }))
}

fn is_rgba(s: &str) -> bool {
    let inner = s.strip_prefix("rgba(").or_else(|| s.strip_prefix("rgb(")).and_then(|r| r.strip_suffix(')'));
    let Some(inner) = inner else { return false };
    let parts: Vec<&str> = inner.split(',').map(str::trim).collect();
    let channel = |p: &str| !p.is_empty() && p.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()) && p.parse::<u16>().is_ok_and(|v| v <= 255);
    let alpha = |p: &str| p.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit() || b == b'.') && p.parse::<f64>().is_ok_and(|v| (0.0..=1.0).contains(&v));
    matches!(parts.len(), 3 | 4) && parts[..3].iter().all(|p| channel(p)) && parts.get(3).is_none_or(|p| alpha(p))
}

fn parse_colors(value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, String>, Failure> {
    let Some(value) = value else { return Ok(BTreeMap::new()) };
    let Value::Object(map) = value else { return Err(Failure::invalid("colors")) };
    let mut colors = BTreeMap::new();
    for (key, v) in map {
        if MEANINGS.iter().any(|(name, _)| key.starts_with(name)) {
            continue;
        }
        let line = LINE_COLORS.contains(&key.as_str());
        if !line && !SOLID_COLORS.contains(&key.as_str()) {
            return Err(Failure::invalid(format!("colors.{key}")));
        }
        let color =
            v.as_str().map(str::to_ascii_lowercase).filter(|c| !c.contains("var(") && (parse_hex(c).is_some() || (line && is_rgba(c))));
        colors.insert(key.clone(), color.ok_or_else(|| Failure::invalid(format!("colors.{key}")))?);
    }
    Ok(colors)
}

### Task 4: `look.rs`, part 3: the look

**Model: sonnet**

**Files:**
- Modify: `src-tauri/src/look.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 2 and 3.
- Produces: `pub struct Check { a, b: String, ratio: f64, min: f64, pass: bool }`; `pub struct Look { vars: BTreeMap<String, String>, scheme: &'static str, motion: String, effects: Effects, checks: Vec<Check>, notices: Vec<String>, appearance: Appearance }` (all serialized as they are) with `Look::failing() -> Option<Failure>` (key `error.look.pairFailed`, the failing pairs joined as `a / b, a / b` in the detail); `pub fn look(&Appearance) -> Result<Look, Failure>`; `pub fn validated(&Obj) -> Result<(Appearance, Look), Failure>` (parses, computes, and refuses a failing pair: the trust boundary of `save_settings`); `pub fn preview(&Obj) -> Result<Look, Failure>` (a failing pair is reported inside the look); `pub fn stored(Option<&Obj>) -> Look` (never fails: an absent, old or failing appearance gives the default look and a line on stderr).

How `look()` works, in the spec's order: the base map with its overrides; a ground's frame, surfaces, lines, inks and meaning colours; the user's `colors` (a changed `bg` brings the meaning colours of Sala or Manhã, whichever reads better, and pushes the plain inks in 5% steps until they pass); the accent through `accent_for`; the fonts; `textSize` (`--fs-ui`, reading text is `--fs-ui` + 1.5 px); `density` (`--dens`); every radius token times `corners`; the three effect switches (`texture` off sets `--grain` to 0 and `--bench` to none, `glow` off flattens `--focus-light` and `--amber-light`, `ambient` off sets the three `--amb-*` tokens to none); then the 26 required pairs. The inks checked are `ink`, `ink-2`, `ink-3`, `amber-ink`, `claude-ink` and `codex-ink`, each on `bg`, `s1`, `s2` and `s3` at 4.5:1, plus `accent` on `bg` at 3:1 and `accent-ink` on `accent` at 4.5:1.

**Proof commands that must pass at the end:** `cargo test -p faber look::` (18 tests).

    #[test]
    fn every_factory_look_passes_all_twenty_six_pairs_and_sets_every_variable() {
        let base_keys: Vec<String> = strings(token("/css/base")).into_keys().collect();
        for id in bases() {
            let look = look(&Appearance::of(id)).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(look.checks.len(), 26, "{id}");
            assert_eq!(failing_names(&look), Vec::<String>::new(), "{id}");
            assert_eq!(look.vars.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>(), base_keys, "{id}: a look must set every variable");

    }

    #[test]
    fn the_contrast_table_is_recomputed_from_the_css_section() {
        let mut lowest_plain = f64::MAX;
        let mut lowest_meaning = f64::MAX;
        for id in bases() {
            let look = look(&Appearance::of(id)).unwrap();
            let table = token("/contrast").get(id).and_then(Value::as_object).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no table for {id}"));
            assert_eq!(table.len(), 26, "{id}");
            for c in &look.checks {
                let listed = table[&format!("{} / {}", c.a, c.b)].as_f64().unwrap();
                assert!((listed - c.ratio).abs() <= 0.01, "{id}: {} / {} lists {listed}, measures {}", c.a, c.b, c.ratio);
                if c.a.starts_with("ink") {
                    lowest_plain = lowest_plain.min(c.ratio);
                } else if c.a.ends_with("-ink") && c.a != "accent-ink" {
                    lowest_meaning = lowest_meaning.min(c.ratio);
                }
            }
        }
        assert_eq!((lowest_plain, lowest_meaning), (4.59, 5.39));
        let sala = look(&sala()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sala.vars["accent-ink"], "#0e0e10", "a factory look keeps its own accent ink");
        assert_eq!(sala.checks.last().map(|c| c.ratio), Some(16.93));
    }

}

// ---- the look ----

/// One of the required contrast pairs, measured.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Check {
    pub a: String,
    pub b: String,
    pub ratio: f64,
    pub min: f64,
    pub pass: bool,
}

/// What the screen applies: the CSS variables (without the leading `--`), the motion level the user chose, the
/// effect switches, the contrast checks and any notices. `appearance` is the choices with every field filled in
/// from the base, so the screen can show which control is on.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Look {
    pub vars: Vars,
    pub scheme: &'static str,
    pub motion: String,
    pub effects: Effects,
    pub checks: Vec<Check>,
    pub notices: Vec<String>,
    pub appearance: Appearance,
}

impl Look {
    /// A look with a failing pair cannot be saved or applied. The failure names every failing pair.
    pub fn failing(&self) -> Option<Failure> {
        let names: Vec<String> = self.checks.iter().filter(|c| !c.pass).map(|c| format!("{} / {}", c.a, c.b)).collect();
        (!names.is_empty()).then(|| Failure::with("error.look.pairFailed", names.join(", ")))
    }
}

fn ink_min() -> f64 {
    number(token("/validator/required-pairs/ink-min"))
}

fn required_checks(vars: &Vars) -> Vec<Check> {
    let pairs = token("/validator/required-pairs");
    let inks = [list("/validator/required-pairs/plain-inks"), list("/validator/required-pairs/meaning-inks")].concat();
    let mut wanted: Vec<(&str, &str, f64)> = Vec::new();
    for ink in &inks {
        for surface in list("/validator/required-pairs/surfaces") {
            wanted.push((ink, surface, ink_min()));
        }
    }
    for key in ["accent", "accent-ink"] {
        let p = &pairs[key];
        wanted.push((p[0].as_str().expect("pair"), p[1].as_str().expect("pair"), number(&p[2])));
    }
    wanted
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(a, b, min)| {
            let r = ratio(rgb_of(vars, a), rgb_of(vars, b));
            Check { a: a.into(), b: b.into(), ratio: (r * 100.0).round() / 100.0, min, pass: r >= min }
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Whole pixels from a value like `12px`.
fn px(value: &str) -> f64 {
    value.trim_end_matches("px").parse().unwrap_or(0.0)
}

pub fn look(a: &Appearance) -> Result<Look, Failure> {
    let mut vars = css_of(&a.base).ok_or_else(|| Failure::invalid("base"))?;
    let own = vars.clone();
    let defaults = &token("/defaults")[a.base.as_str()];
    let mut notices = Vec::new();

    // A ground replaces the surfaces, lines, inks and meaning colours; the base keeps everything else.
    let ground = a.ground.as_deref().filter(|g| *g != a.base);
    if let Some(g) = ground.and_then(css_of) {
        for key in list("/validator/ground-keys") {
            if let Some(v) = g.get(key) {
                vars.insert(key.into(), v.clone());
            }
        }
    }

    // Colours of the user. A new `bg` brings the meaning colours of a dark or a light ground and pushes the plain
    // inks until they read on every surface; anything else that fails is reported by the checks.
    let bg_before = vars["bg"].clone();
    vars.extend(a.colors.clone());
    let bg_changed = vars["bg"] != bg_before;
    if bg_changed {
        let bg = rgb_of(&vars, "bg");
        let (source, to) = if white_reads_better(bg) { ("sala", WHITE) } else { ("manha", BLACK) };
        let source = css_of(source).expect("sala and manha exist");
        for key in list("/validator/meaning-keys") {
            vars.insert(key.into(), source[key].clone());
        }
        let surfaces: Vec<Rgb> = list("/validator/required-pairs/surfaces").into_iter().map(|k| rgb_of(&vars, k)).collect();
        for key in list("/validator/required-pairs/plain-inks") {
            let pushed = fix(rgb_of(&vars, key), &surfaces, ink_min(), to);
            vars.insert(key.into(), hex(pushed));
        }
    }

    // The accent is validated when the user picks one, and again when a new ground or `bg` could make the base's own unreadable.
    if a.accent.is_some() || ground.is_some() || bg_changed {
        let candidate = a.accent.as_deref().and_then(parse_hex).unwrap_or_else(|| rgb_of(&vars, "accent"));
        let accent = accent_for(candidate, rgb_of(&vars, "bg"))?;
        if a.accent.is_some() && accent.fill != candidate {
            notices.push("look.accentAdjusted".to_string());
        }
        vars.insert("accent".into(), hex(accent.fill));
        vars.insert("accent-text".into(), hex(accent.text));
        vars.insert("accent-ink".into(), hex(accent.ink));
    }

    if let Some(font) = &a.text_font {
        vars.insert("font-ui".into(), token("/user-controls/text-fonts")[font.as_str()].as_str().expect("font stack").into());
    }
    if let Some(font) = &a.heading_font {
        vars.extend(strings(&token("/user-controls/heading-fonts")[font.as_str()]));
    }
    if let Some(size) = a.text_size {
        vars.insert("fs-ui".into(), format!("{size}px"));
        vars.insert("fs-read".into(), format!("{}px", size + 1.5));
    }
    if let Some(density) = a.density {
        vars.insert("dens".into(), density.to_string());
    }
    if let Some(corners) = a.corners {
        for key in ["r-xs", "r-sm", "r-md", "r-lg", "r-pill", "r-stage"] {
            vars.insert(key.into(), format!("{}px", (px(&vars[key]) * corners).round()));
        }
    }

    let effects = a.effects.unwrap_or(ALL_EFFECTS);
    if !effects.texture {
        vars.insert("grain".into(), "0".into());
        vars.insert("bench".into(), "none".into());
    }
    if !effects.glow {
        for key in ["focus-light", "amber-light"] {
            vars.insert(key.into(), "0 0 0 0 transparent".into());
        }
    }
    if !effects.ambient {
        for key in ["amb-beam", "amb-loom", "amb-flow"] {
            vars.insert(key.into(), "none".into());
        }
    }

    let checks = required_checks(&vars);
    let text = |field: &Option<String>, key: &str| field.clone().or_else(|| defaults[key].as_str().map(String::from));
    let appearance = Appearance {
        base: a.base.clone(),
        ground: Some(ground.unwrap_or(&a.base).to_string()),
        accent: Some(a.accent.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| own["accent"].clone())),
        text_font: text(&a.text_font, "textFont"),
        heading_font: text(&a.heading_font, "headingFont"),
        text_size: Some(a.text_size.unwrap_or_else(|| px(&own["fs-ui"]))),
        density: Some(a.density.unwrap_or_else(|| own["dens"].parse().unwrap_or(1.0))),
        corners: Some(a.corners.unwrap_or(1.0)),
        motion: text(&a.motion, "motion"),
        effects: Some(effects),
        colors: a.colors.clone(),
    };
    let scheme = if white_reads_better(rgb_of(&vars, "bg")) { "dark" } else { "light" };
    Ok(Look { vars, scheme, motion: appearance.motion.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "full".into()), effects, checks, notices, appearance })
}

/// A look that can be saved: the choices parse, the accent passes the validator and every required pair passes.
pub fn validated(map: &Obj) -> Result<(Appearance, Look), Failure> {
    let appearance = Appearance::parse(map, false)?;
    let look = look(&appearance)?;
    match look.failing() {
        Some(f) => Err(f),
        None => Ok((appearance, look)),
    }
}

/// The look for the preview: failing pairs are reported in the look, not as an error.
pub fn preview(map: &Obj) -> Result<Look, Failure> {
    look(&Appearance::parse(map, false)?)
}

/// The look of the saved appearance. An absent, old or failing appearance gives the default look, so the app never
/// fails to start because of its look.
pub fn stored(appearance: Option<&Obj>) -> Look {
    let saved = appearance.and_then(|map| match validated(map) {
        Ok((_, look)) => Some(look),
        Err(f) => {
            eprintln!("faber: the saved appearance was ignored: {} {}", f.key, f.detail.unwrap_or_default());
            None
        }
    });
    saved.unwrap_or_else(|| look(&Appearance::of(default_base())).expect("the default look is valid"))
}

}

// ---- the theme file ----

/// What `open_theme` hands to the screen: the file's name and its choices.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct ThemeFile {
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(flatten)]
    pub appearance: Appearance,
}

fn valid_name(name: &str) -> bool {
    (1..=80).contains(&name.chars().count())
}

/// Reads a theme file. A file holds choices, never CSS: `var(` is refused and so are unknown keys, and a refused
/// accent refuses the whole file. Failing pairs do not: the preview names them.
pub fn parse_theme_file(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<ThemeFile, Failure> {
    if bytes.len() as u64 > max_theme_bytes() {
        return Err(Failure::key("error.look.fileTooBig"));
    }
    let Ok(Value::Object(map)) = serde_json::from_slice::<Value>(bytes) else { return Err(Failure::invalid("file")) };
    if map.get("format").and_then(Value::as_str) != Some("faber-theme") {
        return Err(Failure::invalid("format"));
    }
    if map.get("version").and_then(Value::as_u64) != Some(1) {
        return Err(Failure::invalid("version"));
    }
    let name = map.get("name").and_then(Value::as_str).filter(|n| valid_name(n)).ok_or_else(|| Failure::invalid("name"))?;
    let appearance = Appearance::parse(&map, true)?;
    look(&appearance)?;
    Ok(ThemeFile { name: name.into(), appearance })
}

#[derive(Serialize)]
struct FileOut<'a> {
    format: &'static str,
    version: u8,
    name: &'a str,
    #[serde(flatten)]
    appearance: &'a Appearance,
}

/// The theme file for a look that can be saved, with its keys in a stable order.
pub fn write_theme_file(appearance: &Obj, name: &str) -> Result<String, Failure> {
    if !valid_name(name) {
        return Err(Failure::invalid("name"));
    }
    let (appearance, _) = validated(appearance)?;
    let out = FileOut { format: "faber-theme", version: 1, name, appearance: &appearance };
    Ok(serde_json::to_string_pretty(&out).expect("plain data always serializes") + "\n")
}

/// The window's first paint: the look's `frame` colour.
pub fn frame_color(look: &Look) -> Rgb {
    rgb_of(&look.vars, "frame")
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_settings_write_that_fails_keeps_the_saved_settings_and_changes_nothing_live() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let f = fake(fixture("claude", "chat"));
    let (engine, _rx) = engine(dir.path(), Vendor::Claude, f.spawn.clone());
    let project = engine.add_project(&dir.path().to_string_lossy());
    let saved = Settings {
        appearance: serde_json::json!({ "base": "oficina" }).as_object().cloned(),
        user_hooks: Some(false),
        language: Some("pt-BR".into()),
        ..Settings::default()
    };
    engine.save_settings(saved.clone()).unwrap();
    let file = dir.path().join("settings.json");
    let bytes = std::fs::read(&file).unwrap();
    let warm =
        WarmRequest { conversation_id: None, area: Area::Project, project_id: Some(project.id.clone()), choice: choice("claude", None) };
    engine.warm(warm.clone());
    let started = f.args.lock().unwrap().len();

    // A directory where the temp file goes makes the write fail while settings.json stays valid.
    let tmp = dir.path().join("settings.json.tmp");
    std::fs::create_dir(&tmp).unwrap();
    let patch = Settings { user_hooks: Some(true), language: Some("de".into()), ..Settings::default() };
    assert_eq!(engine.save_settings(patch.clone()).unwrap_err(), "error.settings.saveFailed");
    assert_eq!(std::fs::read(&file).unwrap(), bytes);
    assert_eq!(engine.state().settings, saved);
    engine.warm(warm.clone());
    assert_eq!(f.args.lock().unwrap().len(), started, "the failed save restarted an idle project CLI");

    std::fs::remove_dir(&tmp).unwrap();
    assert!(!tmp.exists());
    let after = engine.save_settings(patch).unwrap();
    assert_eq!((after.user_hooks, after.language.as_deref()), (Some(true), Some("de")));
    assert_eq!(engine.state().settings, after);
    engine.shutdown();
}

fn write<T: Serialize>(path: &Path, value: &T) {
    let tmp = path.with_extension("json.tmp");
    let json = serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).expect("plain data always serializes");

```rust
}

/// Writes through a temp file. On failure the temp file is removed and the error returned; the old file stays as it was.
fn write<T: Serialize>(path: &Path, value: &T) -> io::Result<()> {
    let tmp = path.with_extension("json.tmp");
    let json = serde_json::to_string_pretty(value).expect("plain data always serializes");
    fs::write(&tmp, json).and_then(|()| fs::rename(&tmp, path)).inspect_err(|_| drop(fs::remove_file(&tmp)))
}
```

### Task 7: The shell: `look` in `state`, `preview_look`, a validating `save_settings`, theme files and the first paint

**Model: sonnet**

**Files:**
- Modify: `src-tauri/src/main.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `look::{Failure, Look, ThemeFile, stored, preview, validated, write_theme_file, parse_theme_file, theme_extension, max_theme_bytes, frame_color}` (Tasks 2 to 5) and `Engine::save_settings`, `Engine::settings` (Task 6).
- Produces, for the screen (Task 8 types them):
  - `state()` returns `FullState` with a new field `look` (the look of the saved appearance).
  - `preview_look(appearance) -> Look` (rejects with a `Failure`).
  - `save_settings(patch) -> SavedSettings { settings, look }`. When the patch has `appearance`, it is validated first (a trust boundary, because a theme file comes from other people): a refused look saves nothing and rejects with its `Failure`; a failed write rejects with the key `error.settings.saveFailed`.
  - `save_theme(appearance, name) -> bool` (false when the person cancels the dialog) and `open_theme() -> ThemeFile | null`, both through the dialog plugin; a file larger than the limit is refused after reading one byte too many, not after loading it.
  - The window's background is the look's `frame` colour, so the first paint already has the theme.

**Proof commands that must pass at the end:** `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo clippy -p faber --features test-hooks -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p faber` (29 tests: the 24 of `look.rs` and the 5 that were already there) and `cargo test -p faber-engine`.

The commands are thin: every rule they apply is `look.rs` and has its tests. They are exercised end to end by Task 15's scenarios.

Replace it with:

```rust
}

/// The look of an appearance that is not saved. Failing pairs come back inside the look.
#[tauri::command]
async fn preview_look(appearance: Map<String, Value>) -> Result<Look, Failure> {
    look::preview(&appearance)
}

/// A theme file comes from other people, so an appearance is checked here before anything is saved.
#[tauri::command]
async fn save_settings(faber: State<'_, Faber>, mut patch: Settings) -> Result<SavedSettings, Failure> {
    if let Some(map) = &patch.appearance {
        patch.appearance = Some(look::validated(map)?.0.to_map());
    }
    let settings = faber.engine.save_settings(patch)?;
    let look = look::stored(settings.appearance.as_ref());
    Ok(SavedSettings { settings, look })
}

#[tauri::command]
async fn save_theme(app: AppHandle, appearance: Map<String, Value>, name: String) -> Result<bool, Failure> {
    let json = look::write_theme_file(&appearance, &name)?;
    let mut dialog = app.dialog().file().add_filter("JSON", &["json"]).set_file_name(format!("my-theme{}", look::theme_extension()));
    if let Some(w) = main_window(&app) {
        dialog = dialog.set_parent(&w);
    }
    let Some(target) = dialog.blocking_save_file() else { return Ok(false) };
    std::fs::write(target.into_path().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?, json).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(true)
}

#[tauri::command]
async fn open_theme(app: AppHandle) -> Result<Option<ThemeFile>, Failure> {
    let mut dialog = app.dialog().file().add_filter("JSON", &["json"]);
    if let Some(w) = main_window(&app) {
        dialog = dialog.set_parent(&w);
    }
    let Some(picked) = dialog.blocking_pick_file() else { return Ok(None) };
    // One byte past the limit is enough to refuse a file without loading it.
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    std::fs::File::open(picked.into_path().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?)
        .and_then(|f| f.take(look::max_theme_bytes() + 1).read_to_end(&mut bytes))
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    look::parse_theme_file(&bytes).map(Some)
}
```

// The shapes the look engine sends and takes (src-tauri/src/look.rs). The screen never computes a colour.

export interface Effects {
  ambient: boolean;
  texture: boolean;

/** The user's choices behind a look. Only `base` is required; the rest falls back to the base's own value. */
export interface Appearance {

  accent?: string;

  headingFont?: string;
  textSize?: number;

  min: number;
  pass: boolean;
}
export interface Look {
  /** CSS custom properties, without the leading dashes. */
  vars: Record<string, string>;
  scheme: 'dark' | 'light';
  motion: Motion;
  effects: Effects;
  checks: Check[];
  /** Translation keys, such as `look.accentAdjusted`. */
  notices: string[];
  /** The choices with every field filled in from the base, so a control can show what is on. */
  appearance: Required<Omit<Appearance, 'colors'>> & { colors?: Record<string, string> };
}
/** A refusal from the look engine: a translation key and the field or pairs it is about. */
export interface Failure {
  key: string;
  detail?: string;
}
export type ThemeFile = Appearance & { name: string };

export interface Settings {
  appearance?: Appearance;
  userHooks?: boolean;
  language?: string;

```typescript
};

/** The engine reports user-facing errors as translation keys; Tauri wraps them as plain strings. The look engine
 * rejects with a `Failure` instead, so its error can name the field or the pairs. */
export const failureOf = (err: unknown): Failure => {
  if (typeof err === 'object' && err !== null && 'key' in err && typeof err.key === 'string') return err as Failure;
  return { key: typeof err === 'string' ? err : err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
};
export const errorText = (err: unknown) => failureOf(err).key;
```

**Edit `ui/i18n.ts`, change 2 of 2.** Find this text, which occurs exactly once:

```typescript

/** Texts that arrive from the engine as keys (errors, routing reasons); anything else is shown as it came. */
export function fromEngine(text: string | undefined): string {
  if (!text) return '';
  // Auto's reason, "reason.route.<kind>.<level>": the kind of task and how hard it is.
  const route = /^reason\.route\.(\w+)\.(\w+)$/.exec(text);
  if (route) return t('reason.route', { kind: t(`kind.${route[1]}` as Key), level: t(`level.${route[2]}` as Key) });
  return text in en ? t(text as Key) : text;
}
```

Replace it with:

```typescript

/** Texts that arrive from the engine as keys (errors, routing reasons); anything else is shown as it came. */
export function fromEngine(text: string | undefined, vars: Record<string, string | number> = {}): string {
  if (!text) return '';
  // Auto's reason, "reason.route.<kind>.<level>": the kind of task and how hard it is.
  const route = /^reason\.route\.(\w+)\.(\w+)$/.exec(text);
  if (route) return t('reason.route', { kind: t(`kind.${route[1]}` as Key), level: t(`level.${route[2]}` as Key) });
  return text in en ? t(text as Key, vars) : text;
}

/** The sentence for a refusal: the shell's key, with the field or the colour pairs it is about as `{detail}`. */
export function failureText(err: unknown): string {
  const { key, detail } = failureOf(err);
  return fromEngine(key, detail === undefined ? {} : { detail });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Check**

Run from the repository root (Node 24 reads the TypeScript files directly):

```bash
node --input-type=module - <<'EOF'
const texts = {
  en: (await import('./ui/locales/en.ts')).en,
  'pt-BR': (await import('./ui/locales/pt-BR.ts')).ptBR,
  es: (await import('./ui/locales/es.ts')).es,
  fr: (await import('./ui/locales/fr.ts')).fr,
  de: (await import('./ui/locales/de.ts')).de,
};
// A text is a sentence, or an object with the plural forms of a sentence (`one`, `other`).
const flat = (text) => (typeof text === 'string' ? text : Object.values(text ?? {}).join(' '));
const holes = (text) => [...new Set([...flat(text).matchAll(/\{(\w+)\}/g)].map((m) => m[1]))].sort().join();
let bad = 0;
for (const [code, messages] of Object.entries(texts)) {
  for (const key of Object.keys(texts.en)) {
    const text = messages[key];
    if (flat(text).trim() === '') {
      console.error(`FAIL ${code}: ${key} is missing or empty`);
      bad += 1;
    } else if (holes(text) !== holes(texts.en[key])) {
      console.error(`FAIL ${code}: ${key} has other {placeholders} than English`);
      bad += 1;
    }
  }
  for (const key of Object.keys(messages)) {
    if (!(key in texts.en)) {
      console.error(`FAIL ${code}: ${key} is not an English key`);
      bad += 1;
    }
  }
}
for (const key of ['look.sala.name', 'look.alto.desc', 'look.accent.pine', 'error.look.nearAmber', 'error.settings.saveFailed', 'ambient.idea']) {
  if (!(key in texts.en)) {
    console.error(`FAIL en: ${key} is missing`);
    bad += 1;
  }
}
for (const key of Object.keys(texts.en)) {
  if (/^set\.(preset|mode|uiFont|readFont)|^set\.accent\./.test(key)) {
    console.error(`FAIL en: ${key} belongs to the old Mono model`);
    bad += 1;
  }
}
console.log(bad ? `locales NOT ok (${bad})` : `locales ok: ${Object.keys(texts.en).length} texts in 5 languages`);
process.exit(bad ? 1 : 0);
EOF
```

Expected: `locales ok: 198 texts in 5 languages`. A `FAIL` line names the language and the key.

- [ ] **Step 4: Check**

```bash
node --input-type=module - <<'EOF'
import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';

const css = readFileSync('ui/fonts.css', 'utf8');
let bad = 0;
const need = (path, what) => {
  if (!existsSync(path)) {
    console.error(`FAIL ${what} does not exist: ${path}`);
    bad += 1;
  }
};
const imports = [...css.matchAll(/@import '([^']+)'/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
const urls = [...css.matchAll(/url\(([^)]+)\)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
for (const spec of imports) need(resolve('node_modules', spec), '@import');
for (const url of urls) need(resolve(dirname('ui/fonts.css'), url), 'url()');
const families = new Set([...css.matchAll(/font-family: '([^']+)'/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
for (const family of ['Archivo', 'Newsreader', 'Bodoni Moda', 'Geist Mono']) {
  if (!families.has(family)) {
    console.error(`FAIL no @font-face for ${family}`);
    bad += 1;
  }
}
if (/cyrillic|vietnamese|greek/.test(css)) {
  console.error('FAIL only the latin and latin-ext subsets belong here');
  bad += 1;
}
console.log(bad ? `fonts NOT ok (${bad})` : `fonts ok: ${imports.length} imports, ${urls.length} font files`);
process.exit(bad ? 1 : 0);
EOF
```

Expected: `fonts ok: 20 imports, 14 font files`. The stylesheet is not used by the page until Task 14 (`main.tsx`).
